========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hey, yeah, thanks. I guess I'm here because... well, I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's getting really bad. I’m snapping on my operations calls at work, I'm snapping at my wife and kids, and that is just not how I’ve ever handled things. I'm usually the calm one, you know? 

Like, I manage operations, so you have to be level-headed. Just Tuesday, we had this supplier issue out of Ohio—well, technically it was Kentucky, right on the border, near Cincinnati. I drove through there once back in, what, 2004? The traffic was an absolute nightmare because of some construction on the bridge. Took me three hours just to cross the river, and my AC was busted at the time. But anyway, this supplier was maybe ten minutes late with an update, and I just completely lost my mind on the guy on the conference call. Just screamed at him. 

And then later my wife asked me to pick up the dry cleaning, which, by the way, the place we go to just raised their prices again. Five dollars to press a standard button-down shirt! Can you believe that? It's highway robbery, honestly, I should just start ironing them myself like I did in high school. But... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. She just asked about the dry cleaning and I completely bit her head off. I don't know what's going on with me, but I can't keep snapping like this.

Yeah, exactly, like I was just saying. It's pretty much constant lately. I just keep blowing up at everyone over absolutely nothing. Snapping on those operations calls, snapping at my family... and like I said, that is just not how I’ve ever handled things in my life. I'm a manager, you know? You can't act like that. 

But now, it's like every little thing sets me off. Even inanimate objects! Like yesterday morning, I was making coffee. I bought this new drip machine, one of those fancy ones with the programmable timer. My brother-in-law recommended it to me. He's always buying these expensive gadgets. Honestly, his garage is just packed full of junk he used exactly one time. He bought this commercial-grade power washer last summer, and I swear he used it on his driveway for maybe ten minutes before he snapped the nozzle off. Just left it sitting out there rusting for months. I told him he should at least put a tarp over it, but you know how some people are. 

But anyway, the coffee pot beeped to say it was done brewing, and I just... I slammed my hand on the counter so hard I bruised my palm. Over a beep! So, yeah... I guess irritable is definitely the word for it.

Anxious? I mean... I don't really know if I'd call it anxious. I just feel so wound up all the time. But anxious... like, worrying about things? Not exactly. I don't sit around chewing my fingernails, you know? My cousin Greg, now he's an anxious guy. He literally can't even go to the grocery store without making a spreadsheet first. I went with him once to pick up some stuff for a barbecue—this was back when my uncle was still living in that big house out by the lake. Beautiful property, really. Had this huge wrap-around porch that faced the water. We used to go out there every Fourth of July and set off fireworks right off the dock. One year, my idiot brother accidentally knocked a whole box of Roman candles into the water. We spent like an hour trying to fish them out with a pool skimmer. 

But yeah, Greg was just pacing up and down the condiment aisle because they didn't have the exact brand of mustard he wrote down on his spreadsheet. It was ridiculous. So, no, I'm not like that. I don't really know if I feel anxious, I just feel... I don't know, like I'm about to jump out of my skin all the time.

No, no, that's what I was saying earlier. I'm usually the calm one. This is definitely new. It's been going on for, I don't know, about three weeks now? Ever since... well, ever since that valve failure at the facility. The one where a guy got hurt. 

We had this massive pressure buildup on line four, which, honestly, I've been telling corporate for two years that those valves needed upgrading. Two years! I sent them the specs, I got quotes from three different vendors... one of them was this guy out of Texas, actually. Dallas, I think. Or maybe Fort Worth. He came up here to do a walk-through of the plant and he was wearing these ridiculous cowboy boots. Like, real snakeskin or something. On a factory floor! I had to make him put on these oversized steel-toe covers that made him look like he was wearing clown shoes. It was hilarious. He kept tripping over his own feet near the loading dock. 

But anyway, yeah, they didn't listen to me about the valves, and then three weeks ago, one blew. And ever since that happened and the guy got injured, I just... I can't shake this edge. I'm just snapping at everyone.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Detail-focused? I don't know about that. Tangents? I'm just telling you what happened. I mean, maybe I talk a little more than I used to, but I've always been a pretty thorough guy. You kind of have to be in my line of work. Managing operations, you've got a lot of moving parts. Like, you can't just tell someone to fix a machine, you have to know exactly which part they're replacing, who the vendor is, what the lead time is... 

Speaking of lead times, the supply chain right now is an absolute nightmare. I ordered these specialized gaskets back in October, and they didn't show up until January. And when they finally got here, the delivery guy backed his truck right into the loading bay door. Just smashed it. The guy claimed he couldn't see out of his mirrors because it was raining, but I'm telling you, he was probably looking at his phone. People are always on their phones now. My daughter, she's sixteen, she literally walks around the house staring at a screen. I told her the other day she's going to walk right into a wall, and sure enough, she tripped over the dog. The dog was fine, he's a Golden Retriever, thick skull, you know? We got him from this breeder down in... oh, where was it? Somewhere near Springfield. 

But anyway, what were you asking? Oh, tangents. No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just trying to give you the full picture of what's going on.

Avoiding things? I mean, I don't think so. I still go to work every day. You kind of have to, right? The bills don't pay themselves. My wife was actually just looking at our electric bill the other day and couldn't believe how high it was. I told her it's because she leaves the space heater running in the sunroom. The whole town basically shut down to drive up to the game. 

Anyway, Jimmy did a great job on the sunroom, but it gets drafty in the winter. So she runs that heater constantly. I told her we should just put in better insulation, maybe some of that spray foam stuff. I saw a video about it online, it expands and fills all the cracks. Looks pretty easy to do yourself, honestly. I've got a buddy who did his whole attic over a weekend. 

But no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything. I'm just going about my normal routine, you know? Just trying to keep things running.

I told him he should at least put a tarp over it, but you know how some people are. \n\nBut anyway, the coffee pot beeped to say it was done brewing, and I just... Guilty? I mean... maybe a little? But mostly I just feel justified in the moment, you know? Because people just aren't doing their jobs right. Like, if you ask someone to file a report by 3:00 PM, it should be done by 3:00 PM. It's not rocket science. It's like this one time at the DMV last month. I was trying to renew my registration, right? And I had all my paperwork. I had the form, the proof of insurance, the emissions test... which, by the way, the emissions testing place I go to is right next to this amazing barbecue joint. Smitty's. Have you ever been to Smitty's? They do this brisket that just melts in your mouth. Smoked for like fourteen hours over hickory wood. The owner, Smitty—obviously—he's this big guy with a beard down to his chest. He actually gave me his secret rub recipe once, well, most of it. He wouldn't tell me the exact ratio of paprika to brown sugar. 

So anyway, I'm at the DMV, and the woman behind the counter tells me I need some other form that wasn't even listed on the website. I mean, how are you supposed to know if they don't put it on the website? The website looks like it was built in 1998 anyway. It's got those terrible drop-down menus that never work right on your phone. I tried to do it on my phone while I was waiting in line at the grocery store, but the screen just kept freezing. 

But yeah, ashamed? I don't know. I just get frustrated when things don't work the way they're supposed to. I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Right before I snap? Honestly, nothing. It's like... it just happens over nothing. That's actually why I'm here. I keep blowing up at people, just snapping on operations calls and even at my family. And that is not how I've ever handled things. I've always been the level-headed one, you know? Like, back when I was managing the night shift at the old plant, we had this massive power outage. Pitch black. And everyone was panicking, but I just grabbed a flashlight and calmly walked everyone out to the muster point. 

Flashlights, by the way, they don't make them like they used to. I bought this tactical one off the internet a few weeks ago, supposed to be military grade, right? Three hundred lumens or whatever. I dropped it once on the driveway—just once—and the lens cracked right down the middle. I tried to return it, but the company’s customer service is basically non-existent. You call the number and it’s just this automated voice that loops forever. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music. Sounded like a synthesizer from an old 80s movie. I love 80s movies, though. *Die Hard*, *Terminator*... classic stuff. 

But yeah, the snapping. It's just out of nowhere. One second I'm fine, the next I'm yelling about something stupid. It's really not like me.

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I do worry. I worry a lot, actually. Because, I mean... I haven't even been cleared to go back to the plant yet, right? And even just working from home, I'm missing things on these operations calls. I'm snapping at people, upsetting my team, and I'm honestly terrified I'm becoming a liability instead of the manager they need me to be. 

And being a liability... that's the worst thing you can be on a site. I remember this one guy we hired back in, what, 2012? Gary. Nice enough guy, but he just wouldn't pay attention during the safety briefings. We had this whole protocol for locking out the machinery before maintenance, the lock-out/tag-out system. It's standard stuff, OSHA required. You have your own personal padlock, and nobody else has the key. Well, Gary bought this cheap knockoff padlock from a hardware store instead of using the company-issued ones. The company ones were brass, heavy duty, made by Master Lock, I think. Master Lock used to have those commercials where they'd shoot the padlock with a rifle and it wouldn't open. Do you remember those? They used to play them all the time during Sunday football. Football just isn't the same anymore, either. Too many flags, too many reviews. You sit there for ten minutes watching a replay in slow motion just to see if a guy's toe touched a blade of white grass. It completely ruins the momentum of the game. My dad and I used to watch the Bears every Sunday, and he'd make this incredible chili. He'd put dark chocolate in it. Sounds crazy, but it gave it this rich, deep flavor...

But yeah, the future. I just... I can't keep upsetting people on these calls. If I can't even manage a Zoom meeting without blowing up, how am I supposed to run a whole operation when I finally go back?

I don't know about that. Tangents? I'm just telling you what happened. I mean, maybe I talk a little more than I used to, but I've always been a pretty thorough guy. You kind of have to be in my line of work. Managing operations, you've got a lot of moving parts. Like, you can't just tell someone to fix a machine, you have to know exactly which part they're replacing, who the vendor is, what the lead time is... \n\nSpeaking of lead times, the supply chain right now is an absolute nightmare. I ordered these specialized gaskets back in October, and they didn't show up until January. And when they finally got here, the delivery guy backed his truck right into the loading bay door. Just smashed it. The guy claimed he couldn't see out of his mirrors because it was raining, but I'm telling you, he was probably looking at his phone. People are always on their phones now. Oh, tangents. No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just trying to give you the full picture of what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain situations or places since the valve failure at the facility?\n\nPatient: Avoiding things? I mean, I don't think so. I still go to work every day. You kind of have to, right? The bills don't pay themselves. My wife was actually just looking at our electric bill the other day and couldn't believe how high it was. I told her it's because she leaves the space heater running in the sunroom. She loves that room. We put it on about five years ago—hired this contractor, Jimmy, who I actually went to high school with. Good guy, played defensive tackle on our varsity team. We made it all the way to the state semi-finals our senior year, which was a huge deal because our town hadn't been to state since the seventies. The whole town basically shut down to drive up to the game. \n\nAnyway, Jimmy did a great job on the sunroom, but it gets drafty in the winter. So she runs that heater constantly. I told her we should just put in better insulation, maybe some of that spray foam stuff. I saw a video about it online, it expands and fills all the cracks. Just trying to keep things running.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed after you snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean... maybe a little? But mostly I just feel justified in the moment, you know? Because people just aren't doing their jobs right. Like, if you ask someone to file a report by 3:00 PM, it should be done by 3:00 PM. It's not rocket science. It's like this one time at the DMV last month. I was trying to renew my registration, right? And I had all my paperwork. I had the form, the proof of insurance, the emissions test... which, by the way, the emissions testing place I go to is right next to this amazing barbecue joint. Smitty's. Have you ever been to Smitty's? They do this brisket that just melts in your mouth. Smoked for like fourteen hours over hickory wood. The owner, Smitty—obviously—he's this big guy with a beard down to his chest. He actually gave me his secret rub recipe once, well, most of it. He wouldn't tell me the exact ratio of paprika to brown sugar. \n\nSo anyway, I'm at the DMV, and the woman behind the counter tells me I need some other form that wasn't even listed on the website. Minecraft, I think? He just sits there building virtual houses for hours. I don't really get it. When I was his age, we were outside building actual treehouses. Me and my brother built this massive two-story one out of scrap lumber we hauled out of the old yard on Route 9. We even rigged up a pulley system with an old bucket to haul up our snacks. My mom used to make these incredible peanut butter and banana sandwiches... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it's just constant. No, no, not at all. Like I said, I've always been the calm one, the level-headed guy. I've never had issues with authority or getting along with people. I've been managing teams for over a decade. You don't get to be an operations manager by fighting with the higher-ups or alienating your crew. I've always respected the chain of command. 

Speaking of the chain of command, it reminds me of my grandfather. He was in the Navy. Served on a destroyer in the Pacific. He used to tell me these stories about the discipline they had on those ships. Everything had to be spotless. They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. It's got the ship's insignia engraved on it. Zippos are great, you know? They have that distinct sound when you open them, that metallic *clink*. You just don't get that with those cheap plastic lighters you buy at the gas station. I used to go to this gas station down on 4th Street, Mac's. Old Mac ran the place for thirty years. He always had a jar of those root beer barrels on the counter. You know the ones? Shaped like little wooden barrels. I broke a tooth on one of those when I was twelve. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...

But yeah, to answer your question, no. I've always gotten along fine with everyone. This whole snapping thing, it's completely new. It's just not me.

Have you ever been to Smitty's? They do this brisket that just melts in your mouth. Smoked for like fourteen hours over hickory wood. The owner, Smitty—obviously—he's this big guy with a beard down to his chest. He actually gave me his secret rub recipe once, well, most of it. He wouldn't tell me the exact ratio of paprika to brown sugar. \n\nSo anyway, I'm at the DMV, and the woman behind the counter tells me I need some other form that wasn't even listed on the website. I mean, how are you supposed to know if they don't put it on the website? The website looks like it was built in 1998 anyway. It's got those terrible drop-down menus that never work right on your phone. I tried to do it on my phone while I was waiting in line at the grocery store, but the screen just kept freezing. \n\nBut yeah, ashamed? I don't know. I just get frustrated when things don't work the way they're supposed to. I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens right before you snap at someone or get irritable?\n\nPatient: Right before I snap? Honestly, nothing. It's like... it just happens over nothing. That's actually why I'm here. I keep blowing up at people, just snapping on operations calls and even at my family. And that is not how I've ever handled things. I've always been the level-headed one, you know? Like, back when I was managing the night shift at the old plant, we had this massive power outage. Pitch black. And everyone was panicking, but I just grabbed a flashlight and calmly walked everyone out to the muster point. \n\nFlashlights, by the way, they don't make them like they used to. I bought this tactical one off the internet a few weeks ago, supposed to be military grade, right? Three hundred lumens or whatever. I dropped it once on the driveway—just once—and the lens cracked right down the middle. I tried to return it, but the company’s customer service is basically non-existent. You call the number and it’s just this automated voice that loops forever. I was on hold for forty-five minutes listening to this awful elevator music. Sounded like a synthesizer from an old 80s movie. I love 80s movies, though. *Die Hard*, *Terminator*... classic stuff. \n\nBut yeah, the snapping. It's just out of nowhere. One second I'm fine, the next I'm yelling about something stupid. It's really not like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're irritable all the time, even when you're not stressed or dealing with a specific problem?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying a minute ago, yeah, it really feels like it's all the time now. It doesn't even take a stressful situation or a real problem. I'm just blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. I'll be on a totally normal operations call, no issues at all, and I'm just snapping at my guys. Or I'll just be sitting in the living room and I snap at my family. And like I told you, that is just not how I've ever handled things. I've always been the calm one. \n\nBut now? I could just be sitting there, trying to use that new expense reporting software they forced on us at work, and my blood just boils. I think it's called Concur? Absolute nightmare to use. You have to upload these receipts, but it won't take PDFs, only JPEGs. Which makes zero sense because every office scanner in the world outputs a PDF. I actually had to call my nephew over to show me how to convert the files. He’s a smart kid, really good with computers. He actually built his own gaming PC from scratch, with the clear glass case and all those neon lights inside. Looks like a spaceship. He plays that game where everything is made of blocks... Minecraft, I think? He just sits there building virtual houses for hours. I don't really get it. When I was his age, we were outside building actual treehouses. Me and my brother built this massive two-story one out of scrap lumber we hauled out of the old yard on Route 9. We even rigged up a pulley system with an old bucket to haul up our snacks. My mom used to make these incredible peanut butter and banana sandwiches... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it's just constant. I'm just irritable all the time over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what might happen in the future when you're feeling irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do worry. I worry a lot, actually. Because, I mean... I haven't even been cleared to go back to the plant yet, right? And even just working from home, I'm missing things on these operations calls. I'm snapping at people, upsetting my team, and I'm honestly terrified I'm becoming a liability instead of the manager they need me to be. \n\nAnd being a liability... that's the worst thing you can be on a site. I remember this one guy we hired back in, what, 2012? Gary. Nice enough guy, but he just wouldn't pay attention during the safety briefings. We had this whole protocol for locking out the machinery before maintenance, the lock-out/tag-out system. It's standard stuff, OSHA required. You have your own personal padlock, and nobody else has the key. Well, Gary bought this cheap knockoff padlock from a hardware store instead of using the company-issued ones. The company ones were brass, heavy duty, made by Master Lock, I think. Master Lock used to have those commercials where they'd shoot the padlock with a rifle and it wouldn't open. Do you remember those? They used to play them all the time during Sunday football. Football just isn't the same anymore, either. Too many flags, too many reviews. You sit there for ten minutes watching a replay in slow motion just to see if a guy's toe touched a blade of white grass. It completely ruins the momentum of the game. My dad and I used to watch the Bears every Sunday, and he'd make this incredible chili. He'd put dark chocolate in it. Sounds crazy, but it gave it this rich, deep flavor...\n\nBut yeah, the future. I just... I can't keep upsetting people on these calls. If I can't even manage a Zoom meeting without blowing up, how am I supposed to run a whole operation when I finally go back?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty getting along with others or had problems with authority figures?\n\nPatient: No, no, not at all. Like I said, I've always been the calm one, the level-headed guy. I've never had issues with authority or getting along with people. I've been managing teams for over a decade. You don't get to be an operations manager by fighting with the higher-ups or alienating your crew. I've always respected the chain of command. \n\nSpeaking of the chain of command, it reminds me of my grandfather. He was in the Navy. Served on a destroyer in the Pacific. He used to tell me these stories about the discipline they had on those ships. Everything had to be spotless. They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. It's got the ship's insignia engraved on it. Zippos are great, you know? They have that distinct sound when you open them, that metallic *clink*. You just don't get that with those cheap plastic lighters you buy at the gas station. I used to go to this gas station down on 4th Street, Mac's. Old Mac ran the place for thirty years. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. You have your own personal padlock, and nobody else has the key. Well, Gary bought this cheap knockoff padlock from a hardware store instead of using the company-issued ones. Do you remember those? They used to play them all the time during Sunday football. Football just isn't the same anymore, either. Too many flags, too many reviews. I just... I can't keep upsetting people on these calls. If I can't even manage a Zoom meeting without blowing up, how am I supposed to run a whole operation when I finally go back?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty getting along with others or had problems with authority figures?\n\nPatient: No, no, not at all. You don't get to be an operations manager by fighting with the higher-ups or alienating your crew. I've always respected the chain of command. \n\nSpeaking of the chain of command, it reminds me of my grandfather. He was in the Navy. Served on a destroyer in the Pacific. He used to tell me these stories about the discipline they had on those ships. Everything had to be spotless. They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess... yeah, sometimes. It's not even like I'm trying to think about it, you know? I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, or trying to answer some emails from home, and suddenly I'm right back there. I see the flash from that valve, I see the smoke filling up the aisle. It just shoves everything else right out of my head. 

It's like... you know those old slide projectors? My parents used to have one. A Kodak Carousel, I think. We'd sit in the living room with the lights off, and my dad would click through slides from our road trips. He loved taking us on road trips. We drove all the way to Mount Rushmore one summer in this beat-up old Ford station wagon. The thing had wood paneling on the sides and these vinyl seats that would absolutely scorch your legs in the summer heat. We didn't have air conditioning, so we just rolled the windows down. You could smell the pine trees when we got out to the Black Hills. My mom bought this little souvenir snow globe at the gift shop there. It had the four presidents in it, and when you shook it, it looked like a blizzard. I always wondered how they got the water inside those things without leaving a bubble at the top. I tried making one once with a baby food jar and some glitter, but it just leaked everywhere and ruined my mom's good lace tablecloth. She was so mad. She had bought that tablecloth at a flea market in... I want to say Ohio? Or maybe Pennsylvania...

But yeah, it's like that. Like a slide just clicks into place in my head, and I'm seeing the flash and the smoke all over again, and then I just... I lose it. I snap.

Physical sensations? Um... I don't know, really. I haven't really noticed anything like that. No pain or anything. I'm usually just so caught up in the... the flash, the smoke, that I'm not paying attention to my body. 

Speaking of not paying attention, my dog, Buster, he is the worst for that. He's a golden retriever mix, and he just has zero spatial awareness. He's always bumping into the coffee table, knocking things over with his tail. That tail is like a whip, I swear. He knocked over this antique lamp my wife inherited from her aunt just last week. It had a wraparound porch and these huge oak trees in the front yard. We used to go up there for Thanksgiving every year. The drive was beautiful in the fall, all the leaves changing colors. We'd always stop at this little farm stand on Route 9 and buy fresh apple cider. The guy who ran it, old man Higgins, he had this vintage tractor parked out front. A John Deere, maybe from the 1950s. It was painted that classic green and yellow. I always wanted a tractor like that when I was a kid. I had this whole collection of die-cast farm animals and tractors. I'd set them up in the dirt behind our garage and play for hours...

But no, to answer your question, I don't think I feel any tension or anything right before I snap. Just the memory, mostly.

They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. It's got the ship's insignia engraved on it. Zippos are great, you know? They have that distinct sound when you open them, that metallic *clink*. You just don't get that with those cheap plastic lighters you buy at the gas station. I used to go to this gas station down on 4th Street, Mac's. Old Mac ran the place for thirty years. He always had a jar of those root beer barrels on the counter. You know the ones? Shaped like little wooden barrels. I broke a tooth on one of those when I was twelve. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. I've always gotten along fine with everyone. This whole snapping thing, it's completely new. My parents used to have one. A Kodak Carousel, I think. We'd sit in the living room with the lights off, and my dad would click through slides from our road trips. He loved taking us on road trips. We drove all the way to Mount Rushmore one summer in this beat-up old Ford station wagon. The thing had wood paneling on the sides and these vinyl seats that would absolutely scorch your legs in the summer heat. We didn't have air conditioning, so we just rolled the windows down. You could smell the pine trees when we got out to the Black Hills. My mom bought this little souvenir snow globe at the gift shop there. It had the four presidents in it, and when you shook it, it looked like a blizzard. I always wondered how they got the water inside those things without leaving a bubble at the top. I tried making one once with a baby food jar and some glitter, but it just leaked everywhere and ruined my mom's good lace tablecloth. She was so mad. She had bought that tablecloth at a flea market in... I want to say Ohio? Or maybe Pennsylvania...\n\nBut yeah, it's like that. Like a slide just clicks into place in my head, and I'm seeing the flash and the smoke all over again, and then I just... I lose it. I snap.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any physical sensations, like tension or pain, when you're about to snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Physical sensations? Um... I don't know, really. Like a Tiffany lamp, but probably a reproduction. Her aunt lived in this massive Victorian house upstate. It had a wraparound porch and these huge oak trees in the front yard. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Traumatic? No, not at all. Nothing like that. Just the valve failure at the plant. Before that, everything was pretty much smooth sailing. 

I actually used to go sailing a lot, speaking of that. My buddy Dave has this 22-foot sailboat down at the marina. We'd go out on Sundays, just to get away from everything. It's so quiet out on the water. Just the wind hitting the sails. The canvas makes this sharp snapping sound when a good gust catches it. Canvas is a really tough material, though. I actually bought this canvas messenger bag a few years ago for work. I figured it would look a little more professional than carrying a backpack, you know, being an operations manager and all. But the strap broke after like two weeks! I was so annoyed. 

I took it back to the store—it was one of those big outdoor recreation places over at the mall—and the cashier gave me the absolute hardest time about returning it because I didn't have the original tags. I mean, who keeps the tags? You cut them off as soon as you get home. I have to use these heavy-duty tin snips to cut through those thick plastic zip-tie things they use for tags nowadays. I keep a pair right on my workbench in the garage. I built that workbench myself, actually, out of some reclaimed barn wood I found on Craigslist. The guy who sold it to me had this massive farm out in the county, raised alpacas I think. Weird looking animals, kind of like little llamas... 

But, uh, no. No other traumas. Just the plant.

We drove all the way to Mount Rushmore one summer in this beat-up old Ford station wagon. The thing had wood paneling on the sides and these vinyl seats that would absolutely scorch your legs in the summer heat. We didn't have air conditioning, so we just rolled the windows down. You could smell the pine trees when we got out to the Black Hills. My mom bought this little souvenir snow globe at the gift shop there. It had the four presidents in it, and when you shook it, it looked like a blizzard. I always wondered how they got the water inside those things without leaving a bubble at the top. I tried making one once with a baby food jar and some glitter, but it just leaked everywhere and ruined my mom's good lace tablecloth. She was so mad. She had bought that tablecloth at a flea market in... I want to say Ohio? Or maybe Pennsylvania...\n\nBut yeah, it's like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, sleep has been... it's been pretty bad, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, and I can hear that warning alarm from the plant just ringing in my head over and over. It's this high-pitched, piercing sound. Kind of like a smoke detector when the battery is dying, you know? That little chirp it does at 3 AM that drives you absolutely crazy. 

I had to replace all the smoke detectors in our house last year because they expired. I didn't even know smoke detectors expired, but apparently they only last like ten years. I ended up buying these smart ones that connect to your Wi-Fi and send alerts to your phone. It was a huge pain to set up because our router is all the way in the basement, and the signal barely reaches the second floor. I had to buy one of those Wi-Fi extender things, but I plugged it into an outlet in the hallway that's controlled by a light switch, so every time my wife turned off the hall light, the internet would drop out. It took me a week to figure out why the Netflix kept buffering right in the middle of our shows. We were watching this documentary series about deep-sea divers, the guys who weld oil rigs underwater. That looks terrifying. Just the pressure down there, and it's pitch black...

But anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to doze off, it's not even real sleep. I just wake up soaking wet in sweat, having these terrible dreams about the smoke and people yelling at the plant. So by the time morning rolls around, I feel like I haven't slept a wink.

She had bought that tablecloth at a flea market in... I want to say Ohio? Or maybe Pennsylvania...\n\nBut yeah, it's like that. Like a slide just clicks into place in my head, and I'm seeing the flash and the smoke all over again, and then I just... I lose it. I snap.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any physical sensations, like tension or pain, when you're about to snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Physical sensations? Um... I don't know, really. I haven't really noticed anything like that. No pain or anything. I'm usually just so caught up in the... the flash, the smoke, that I'm not paying attention to my body. \n\nSpeaking of not paying attention, my dog, Buster, he is the worst for that. He's a golden retriever mix, and he just has zero spatial awareness. He's always bumping into the coffee table, knocking things over with his tail. That tail is like a whip, I swear. He knocked over this antique lamp my wife inherited from her aunt just last week. Shattered the shade completely. It was one of those stained glass ones, you know? Like a Tiffany lamp, but probably a reproduction. Her aunt lived in this massive Victorian house upstate. It had a wraparound porch and these huge oak trees in the front yard. Just the valve failure at the plant. Before that, everything was pretty much smooth sailing. \n\nI actually used to go sailing a lot, speaking of that. Just the plant.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep has been... it's been pretty bad, honestly. That looks terrifying. Just the pressure down there, and it's pitch black...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to doze off, it's not even real sleep. I just wake up soaking wet in sweat, having these terrible dreams about the smoke and people yelling at the plant. So by the time morning rolls around, I feel like I haven't slept a wink.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself preoccupied with thoughts about the valve failure at the facility when you're not actively thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's all the time. I need to get up there on a ladder and clean them out, but my ladder is super wobbly. It's one of those old aluminum extension ladders. My dad gave it to me when my wife and I bought our first house. It's really distracting.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Avoiding places? No, not really. I mean, I still go everywhere I need to go. I haven't changed my routine or anything like that. I still go to the grocery store, the gas station...

It's really not like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're irritable all the time, even when you're not stressed or dealing with a specific problem?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying a minute ago, yeah, it really feels like it's all the time now. It doesn't even take a stressful situation or a real problem. I'm just blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. I'll be on a totally normal operations call, no issues at all, and I'm just snapping at my guys. Or I'll just be sitting in the living room and I snap at my family. And like I told you, that is just not how I've ever handled things. I've always been the calm one. \n\nBut now? I could just be sitting there, trying to use that new expense reporting software they forced on us at work, and my blood just boils. I think it's called Concur? Absolute nightmare to use. You have to upload these receipts, but it won't take PDFs, only JPEGs. Which makes zero sense because every office scanner in the world outputs a PDF. I actually had to call my nephew over to show me how to convert the files. He’s a smart kid, really good with computers. He actually built his own gaming PC from scratch, with the clear glass case and all those neon lights inside. Looks like a spaceship. He plays that game where everything is made of blocks... Minecraft, I think? He just sits there building virtual houses for hours. I don't really get it. When I was his age, we were outside building actual treehouses. Me and my brother built this massive two-story one out of scrap lumber we hauled out of the old yard on Route 9. We even rigged up a pulley system with an old bucket to haul up our snacks. My mom used to make these incredible peanut butter and banana sandwiches... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it's just constant. I'm just irritable all the time over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what might happen in the future when you're feeling irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do worry. I worry a lot, actually. Because, I mean... I haven't even been cleared to go back to the plant yet, right? And even just working from home, I'm missing things on these operations calls. I'm snapping at people, upsetting my team, and I'm honestly terrified I'm becoming a liability instead of the manager they need me to be. \n\nAnd being a liability... that's the worst thing you can be on a site. I remember this one guy we hired back in, what, 2012? Gary. Nice enough guy, but he just wouldn't pay attention during the safety briefings. We had this whole protocol for locking out the machinery before maintenance, the lock-out/tag-out system. It's standard stuff, OSHA required. You have your own personal padlock, and nobody else has the key. Well, Gary bought this cheap knockoff padlock from a hardware store instead of using the company-issued ones. The company ones were brass, heavy duty, made by Master Lock, I think. Master Lock used to have those commercials where they'd shoot the padlock with a rifle and it wouldn't open. Do you remember those? They used to play them all the time during Sunday football. Football just isn't the same anymore, either. Too many flags, too many reviews. You sit there for ten minutes watching a replay in slow motion just to see if a guy's toe touched a blade of white grass. It completely ruins the momentum of the game. My dad and I used to watch the Bears every Sunday, and he'd make this incredible chili. He'd put dark chocolate in it. Sounds crazy, but it gave it this rich, deep flavor...\n\nBut yeah, the future. I just... I can't keep upsetting people on these calls. If I can't even manage a Zoom meeting without blowing up, how am I supposed to run a whole operation when I finally go back?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty getting along with others or had problems with authority figures?\n\nPatient: No, no, not at all. Like I said, I've always been the calm one, the level-headed guy. I've never had issues with authority or getting along with people. I've been managing teams for over a decade. You don't get to be an operations manager by fighting with the higher-ups or alienating your crew. I've always respected the chain of command. \n\nSpeaking of the chain of command, it reminds me of my grandfather. He was in the Navy. Served on a destroyer in the Pacific. He used to tell me these stories about the discipline they had on those ships. Everything had to be spotless. They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. It's got the ship's insignia engraved on it. Zippos are great, you know? They have that distinct sound when you open them, that metallic *clink*. You just don't get that with those cheap plastic lighters you buy at the gas station. I used to go to this gas station down on 4th Street, Mac's. Old Mac ran the place for thirty years. He always had a jar of those root beer barrels on the counter. You know the ones? Shaped like little wooden barrels. I broke a tooth on one of those when I was twelve. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. I've always gotten along fine with everyone. This whole snapping thing, it's completely new. It's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually thinking about something specific right before you snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, sometimes. It's not even like I'm trying to think about it, you know? I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, or trying to answer some emails from home, and suddenly I'm right back there. I see the flash from that valve, I see the smoke filling up the aisle. It just shoves everything else right out of my head. \n\nIt's like... you know those old slide projectors? My parents used to have one. A Kodak Carousel, I think. We'd sit in the living room with the lights off, and my dad would click through slides from our road trips. He loved taking us on road trips. We drove all the way to Mount Rushmore one summer in this beat-up old Ford station wagon. The thing had wood paneling on the sides and these vinyl seats that would absolutely scorch your legs in the summer heat. We didn't have air conditioning, so we just rolled the windows down. You could smell the pine trees when we got out to the Black Hills. My mom bought this little souvenir snow globe at the gift shop there. It had the four presidents in it, and when you shook it, it looked like a blizzard. I always wondered how they got the water inside those things without leaving a bubble at the top. Like a Tiffany lamp, but probably a reproduction. Her aunt lived in this massive Victorian house upstate. It had a wraparound porch and these huge oak trees in the front yard. It was painted that classic green and yellow. I always wanted a tractor like that when I was a kid. I had this whole collection of die-cast farm animals and tractors. I'd set them up in the dirt behind our garage and play for hours...\n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't think I feel any tension or anything right before I snap. Just the memory, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life besides the valve failure at the facility?\n\nPatient: Traumatic? No, not at all. Nothing like that. Just the valve failure at the plant. Before that, everything was pretty much smooth sailing. \n\nI actually used to go sailing a lot, speaking of that. My buddy Dave has this 22-foot sailboat down at the marina. We'd go out on Sundays, just to get away from everything. It's so quiet out on the water. Just the wind hitting the sails. The canvas makes this sharp snapping sound when a good gust catches it. Canvas is a really tough material, though. I actually bought this canvas messenger bag a few years ago for work. I figured it would look a little more professional than carrying a backpack, you know, being an operations manager and all. But the strap broke after like two weeks! I was so annoyed. \n\nI took it back to the store—it was one of those big outdoor recreation places over at the mall—and the cashier gave me the absolute hardest time about returning it because I didn't have the original tags. I keep a pair right on my workbench in the garage. I built that workbench myself, actually, out of some reclaimed barn wood I found on Craigslist. The guy who sold it to me had this massive farm out in the county, raised alpacas I think. Weird looking animals, kind of like little llamas... \n\nBut, uh, no. No other traumas. Just the plant.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep has been... it's been pretty bad, honestly. We had this whole protocol for locking out the machinery before maintenance, the lock-out/tag-out system. It's standard stuff, OSHA required. You have your own personal padlock, and nobody else has the key. Well, Gary bought this cheap knockoff padlock from a hardware store instead of using the company-issued ones. The company ones were brass, heavy duty, made by Master Lock, I think. Master Lock used to have those commercials where they'd shoot the padlock with a rifle and it wouldn't open. Do you remember those? They used to play them all the time during Sunday football. Football just isn't the same anymore, either. Too many flags, too many reviews. You sit there for ten minutes watching a replay in slow motion just to see if a guy's toe touched a blade of white grass. It completely ruins the momentum of the game. My dad and I used to watch the Bears every Sunday, and he'd make this incredible chili. He'd put dark chocolate in it. Sounds crazy, but it gave it this rich, deep flavor...\n\nBut yeah, the future. I just... I can't keep upsetting people on these calls. If I can't even manage a Zoom meeting without blowing up, how am I supposed to run a whole operation when I finally go back?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty getting along with others or had problems with authority figures?\n\nPatient: No, no, not at all. Like I said, I've always been the calm one, the level-headed guy. I've never had issues with authority or getting along with people. I've been managing teams for over a decade. You don't get to be an operations manager by fighting with the higher-ups or alienating your crew. I've always respected the chain of command. \n\nSpeaking of the chain of command, it reminds me of my grandfather. He was in the Navy. Served on a destroyer in the Pacific. He used to tell me these stories about the discipline they had on those ships. Everything had to be spotless. They'd spend hours polishing the brass fittings until you could see your reflection in them. He gave me his old Zippo lighter from when he was in the service. It's got the ship's insignia engraved on it. Zippos are great, you know? They have that distinct sound when you open them, that metallic *clink*. You just don't get that with those cheap plastic lighters you buy at the gas station. I used to go to this gas station down on 4th Street, Mac's. Old Mac ran the place for thirty years. He always had a jar of those root beer barrels on the counter. You know the ones? Shaped like little wooden barrels. I broke a tooth on one of those when I was twelve. Had to go to Dr. Henderson, the dentist. He had this massive fish tank in his waiting room, filled with these neon tetras...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. I've always gotten along fine with everyone. This whole snapping thing, it's completely new. It's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually thinking about something specific right before you snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, sometimes. It's not even like I'm trying to think about it, you know? I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, or trying to answer some emails from home, and suddenly I'm right back there. I see the flash from that valve, I see the smoke filling up the aisle. It just shoves everything else right out of my head. \n\nIt's like... you know those old slide projectors? My parents used to have one. A Kodak Carousel, I think. We'd sit in the living room with the lights off, and my dad would click through slides from our road trips. He loved taking us on road trips. We drove all the way to Mount Rushmore one summer in this beat-up old Ford station wagon. The thing had wood paneling on the sides and these vinyl seats that would absolutely scorch your legs in the summer heat. We didn't have air conditioning, so we just rolled the windows down. You could smell the pine trees when we got out to the Black Hills. My mom bought this little souvenir snow globe at the gift shop there. It had the four presidents in it, and when you shook it, it looked like a blizzard. I always wondered how they got the water inside those things without leaving a bubble at the top. I tried making one once with a baby food jar and some glitter, but it just leaked everywhere and ruined my mom's good lace tablecloth. She was so mad. She had bought that tablecloth at a flea market in... I want to say Ohio? Or maybe Pennsylvania...\n\nBut yeah, it's like that. Like a slide just clicks into place in my head, and I'm seeing the flash and the smoke all over again, and then I just... I lose it. I snap.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any physical sensations, like tension or pain, when you're about to snap at someone?\n\nPatient: Physical sensations? Um... I don't know, really. I haven't really noticed anything like that. No pain or anything. I'm usually just so caught up in the... the flash, the smoke, that I'm not paying attention to my body. \n\nSpeaking of not paying attention, my dog, Buster, he is the worst for that. He's a golden retriever mix, and he just has zero spatial awareness. He's always bumping into the coffee table, knocking things over with his tail. That tail is like a whip, I swear. He knocked over this antique lamp my wife inherited from her aunt just last week. Shattered the shade completely. It was one of those stained glass ones, you know? Like a Tiffany lamp, but probably a reproduction. Her aunt lived in this massive Victorian house upstate. It had a wraparound porch and these huge oak trees in the front yard. We used to go up there for Thanksgiving every year. The drive was beautiful in the fall, all the leaves changing colors. We'd always stop at this little farm stand on Route 9 and buy fresh apple cider. The guy who ran it, old man Higgins, he had this vintage tractor parked out front. A John Deere, maybe from the 1950s. It was painted that classic green and yellow. I always wanted a tractor like that when I was a kid. I had this whole collection of die-cast farm animals and tractors. I'd set them up in the dirt behind our garage and play for hours...\n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't think I feel any tension or anything right before I snap. Just the memory, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life besides the valve failure at the facility?\n\nPatient: Traumatic? No, not at all. Nothing like that. Just the valve failure at the plant. Before that, everything was pretty much smooth sailing. \n\nI actually used to go sailing a lot, speaking of that. My buddy Dave has this 22-foot sailboat down at the marina. We'd go out on Sundays, just to get away from everything. It's so quiet out on the water. Just the wind hitting the sails. The canvas makes this sharp snapping sound when a good gust catches it. Canvas is a really tough material, though. I actually bought this canvas messenger bag a few years ago for work. I figured it would look a little more professional than carrying a backpack, you know, being an operations manager and all. But the strap broke after like two weeks! I was so annoyed. \n\nI took it back to the store—it was one of those big outdoor recreation places over at the mall—and the cashier gave me the absolute hardest time about returning it because I didn't have the original tags. I mean, who keeps the tags? You cut them off as soon as you get home. I have to use these heavy-duty tin snips to cut through those thick plastic zip-tie things they use for tags nowadays. I keep a pair right on my workbench in the garage. I built that workbench myself, actually, out of some reclaimed barn wood I found on Craigslist. The guy who sold it to me had this massive farm out in the county, raised alpacas I think. Weird looking animals, kind of like little llamas... \n\nBut, uh, no. No other traumas. Just the plant.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep has been... it's been pretty bad, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, and I can hear that warning alarm from the plant just ringing in my head over and over. It's this high-pitched, piercing sound. Kind of like a smoke detector when the battery is dying, you know? That little chirp it does at 3 AM that drives you absolutely crazy. \n\nI had to replace all the smoke detectors in our house last year because they expired. I didn't even know smoke detectors expired, but apparently they only last like ten years. I ended up buying these smart ones that connect to your Wi-Fi and send alerts to your phone. It was a huge pain to set up because our router is all the way in the basement, and the signal barely reaches the second floor. I had to buy one of those Wi-Fi extender things, but I plugged it into an outlet in the hallway that's controlled by a light switch, so every time my wife turned off the hall light, the internet would drop out. It took me a week to figure out why the Netflix kept buffering right in the middle of our shows. We were watching this documentary series about deep-sea divers, the guys who weld oil rigs underwater. That looks terrifying. Just the pressure down there, and it's pitch black...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, when I finally do manage to doze off, it's not even real sleep. I just wake up soaking wet in sweat, having these terrible dreams about the smoke and people yelling at the plant. So by the time morning rolls around, I feel like I haven't slept a wink.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself preoccupied with thoughts about the valve failure at the facility when you're not actively thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's all the time. I can just be sitting at the kitchen table, you know, having my morning coffee, or trying to answer some emails from home, and suddenly I’m right back there. I see the flash from that valve and the smoke filling up the aisle. It just shoves everything else completely out of my head. \n\nSpeaking of emails, my laptop has been running so slow lately. I think I might have clicked on a bad link or something. I get these spam emails all the time from places claiming I won a gift card to Home Depot. I love Home Depot, though. I could spend hours in the hardware aisle just looking at all the different types of screws and fasteners. I was there last weekend looking for some lag bolts to fix the deck in the backyard. The wood is starting to rot near the stairs. I think it's because the gutters are clogged and the water just pours right down onto the wood. I need to get up there on a ladder and clean them out, but my ladder is super wobbly. It's one of those old aluminum extension ladders. My dad gave it to me when my wife and I bought our first house. He was always giving us hand-me-down tools. He had this massive collection of vintage Stanley hand planes... \n\nBut yeah, the thoughts just pop into my head out of nowhere, no matter what I'm doing. It's really distracting.\n\nDoctor: Are there any places or situations that you've been avoiding since the valve failure at the facility?\n\nPatient: Avoiding places? No, not really. I mean, I still go everywhere I need to go. I haven't changed my routine or anything like that. I still go to the grocery store, the gas station... \n\nActually, I was at the gas station just yesterday, that big one right off the interstate. They just installed those new pumps with the little TV screens built right into them. It's so loud, you know? You're just standing there trying to pump your gas and suddenly there's this anchor yelling at you about a new breakfast sandwich or the local weather. I don't need a commercial while I'm putting 87 octane in my truck. My truck has been getting terrible gas mileage lately, by the way. I'm pretty sure the fuel filter needs replacing. I tried looking up a tutorial on YouTube to see if I could just swap it out myself in the driveway, but the guy in the video had a 2018 model and mine is a 2015, so the engine layout under the hood was completely different. It drives me crazy how they change the design every couple of years just so you can't fix it yourself and have to take it to the dealership. The dealership always charges an arm and a leg for labor, it's a total racket. Last time I went there, they tried to tell me I needed new brake pads when I had just replaced them myself six months ago with these premium ceramic ones... \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, avoiding things. No. No, I'm not avoiding anywhere. I still go out and do all my normal stuff."
  }
